# Active theory graph

This table describes the current source imports. It is an implementation status
record, not the completed graph proposed in `plan.md`.

| Presentation_Classes | Main | Typed class contracts for coverage, admissibility, and complete recovery; constraints, joint classes, products and sequences, compatible families and directed unions, complete-intermediate composition, determined components, observations, and covered images. The injective class (`injective_presentation_class`) and its identity instance, every value presenting itself (`identity_presentation_class`). Notions and their intrinsic groups own the remaining local contracts. The determined-component subdomain theorem consumes an explicit source boundary. |
| Presentation_Relations | Presentation_Classes | Lifts of independent predicates and intrinsic relations; within-class invariance, shared-subject conjunction, composition through one intermediate class, and complete-intermediate transport. The transport and contract layers derive correspondence and client composition across different classes. Complement is relative to both complete presentation domains. |
| Presentation_Closure | Presentation_Relations | Presentation of complete subject families, recovery within the subject domain, and exact least-fixed-point transport from actual consequence equations on a proved closed support domain. |
| Bootstrap_Relations | Main | Finite extensional relations, exact maps, images, direct and transitive ancestry; shared complete-list witnesses and finite keyed enumeration. A map of a relation's values preserves and reflects its functionality wherever it is injective on the values the relation holds (`map_relation_values_functional_on`; `map_relation_values_functional` its corollary for an injective map), keeps its domain (`map_relation_values_domain`) and maps its range (`map_relation_values_range`). A set is an injective surjection's image of the set of what it reaches (`surjective_image_eq`); an injective map preserves and reflects the reflexive-transitive paths of a relation's image (`rtrancl_injective_image`). A list of an injective map's images of a set's members is the image of an enumeration of the set (`injective_image_enumeration`, moved from `Factor_Stated_Leaves_Counterpart` by task 483); a map of pairs injective on one component and the identity on the other is injective (`renamed_site_injective`, `inj (map_prod h id)`, moved from `Factor_Use_Renaming`; `keyed_value_injective`, `inj (\<lambda>(k,x). (k,f x))`, its mirror, moved from `Factor_Stated_Leaves_Counterpart`). |

| Carrier_Indexes | Main | The index of a carrier by a key, the notion of DECISIONS.md "A refinement applies a notion; an index is one", checked. Locale `carrier_index`: a carrier's content is a relation `holds c q v` from queries to values, claimed under a formation parameter `formed`; a carrier discharges obligation (1) `distinguishes` (the key injective on the queries `Q`; `distinguishes_by_left_inverse` gives it from `unkey (key x)=x`) and obligation (2) `represents` (the member equation at every key: `search (build c) k v ⟷ (∃q∈Q. key q=k ∧ holds c q v)`), and then obtains the laws as theorems: `query_search` and `query_fibre` (one built index answers every query with the carrier's fibre), `unkeyed_search`, `content_determines_search` (equal content, equal search at every key: row and insertion order are not subjects); law 1 is the signature, only the key's type is searched by. `carrier_index_through_key`: an index of the key image searched by the keys is an index of the carrier through a key that distinguishes. Extension `updated_carrier_index` (obligation 4): `update_at`, `update_preserves_else`, `update_represents`; replacement and insertion laws are its instances through the `change` parameter. Extension `native_carrier_index`: a search site exact at every key term and every index (`site_exact`, with `inj present`), `site_query`, `site_refuses_unpresented`; `index_search_sound` is the one-directional form. Obligation (3), one equation per operation a use needs, is a use's own proof from these facts. Corollaries the uses' forms instantiate: `queries_search` (every query of a set found iff each holds some value), `lookup_found` and `lookup_queries_found` (where the search is an optional lookup, a premise `search i k v ⟷ look i k=Some v`: the lookup is `≠ None` iff the query holds some value), `found_pairs` (the found pairs are the keyed image of the content: a carrier's whole-set contract in one step). No simp, intro or code attributes. `carrier_index_search_agrees` (task 249): an index whose search agrees with an index's search on every formed carrier is an index of the same carrier. |
| Established_Premises | HOL-Library.FSet | A check made where its premise is established, the first notion of DECISIONS.md "The in-place refinements apply two notions", checked. Locale `established_premise original premise body`: obligation (1) `exact` (`premise x ⟹ original x = body x`), which a use proves; at an enclosing operation or a constructor's contract a use gets exactness at the established value with its establishing fact. Extension `checked_premise … refusal`: obligation (2) `refused` (`¬premise x ⟹ original x = refusal x`), giving `checked_at_entry` (the code equation `original x = (if premise x then body x else refusal x)`) and `checked_through` (the check hoisted through any consumer `t`). `checked_union`: a union over a family refused by `{||}` outside `P` is `(if P then ffUnion (fimage T Y) else {||})`. A premise on a later argument is instantiated with the constant applied to the arguments before it; the code equation is stated at the premise's arity, and restating an existing one at that arity is a refinement of its constant (DECISIONS.md "What a refinement keeps equal when a recorded state presents the refined constant's code equations"). No simp, intro or code attributes; nothing about cost. |
| Candidate_Generators | HOL-Library.FSet | A generator of the accepted candidates, the second notion of DECISIONS.md "The in-place refinements apply two notions", checked. Locale `candidate_generator space accepts generated`: obligations `sound` (a generated accepted candidate lies in the space) and `complete` (every accepted candidate of the space is generated), giving `accepted_generated` (`ffilter accepts generated = ffilter accepts space`). Extension `tight_candidate_generator`: obligation `tight` (every generated candidate is accepted), giving `generated_accepted` (`generated = ffilter accepts space`). `singleton_tight_generator` (the guarded candidate is the tight generator of a one-candidate space) and `accepted_singleton` (`ffilter accepts {|x|} = (if accepts x then {|x|} else {||})`); the empty space's law is `RRA_Selection.ffilter_empty_set`. No simp, intro or code attributes; nothing about cost. |
| Finite_Functional_Enumeration | RRA_Finite_Artifacts, Finite_Singleton_Selection | Ordered keys enumerate every row of a finite functional relation without imposing an order on its values; the executable selector accepts repeated encodings of the same value. |
| Functional_Enumeration_Indexes | Carrier_Indexes, Finite_Functional_Enumeration | The index notion interpreted in a finite functional relation, formed by `finite_relation_functional`, keyed by the identity: `functional_rows_index` (index `finite_functional_rows`, search `(k,v)∈set rows`, from `finite_functional_rows_exact`) and `functional_option_index` (index `finite_relation_option`, search `f k=Some v`, from `finite_relation_option_correct`); law 1 as an instance, only the keys ordered. A use cites `functional_rows_index.query_search` and the other laws of `carrier_index`. `Finite_Ordered_Representatives` is not interpreted: its map selects the least key of a value's fibre, not the relation's content. |

| Finite_Compatible_Unions | RRA_Finite_Artifacts, Finite_Set_Composition | Full meaning and exact list execution of every functional union of whole finite fragments; support and scope equations preserve complete contributors. |
| Finite_Demand_Selection | Finite_Set_Composition | Selects an uncovered required key with least supplied cost, with exact absence, membership and finite progress laws. |
| Finite_Directed_Contributions | Finite_Compatible_Unions, Finite_Demand_Selection, Candidate_Generators | Demand-directed whole-fragment binding search is exactly the full compatible-union relation restricted to the required domain; branch values, support and completeness remain explicit. | Finite_Candidate_Assembly | Finite_Demand_Selection | Complete sufficient-subset language, selective construction, inclusion minimality, failure and per-part necessity witnesses; cached coverage preserves the constructor function. |
| Finite_Indexed_Candidate_Assembly | Finite_Candidate_Assembly | Exact index maps retain complete requirement subjects, coverage, uncovered witnesses and the full candidate language; filtered coverage can execute directly on indices. |
| Finite_Directed_Execution | Finite_Directed_Execution_Base, Native_Execution_Refinements | Native natural-valued scoped binding search and candidate assembly, with complete finite input and result interfaces. |

| Factor_Finite_Data_Syntax | Factor_Finite_Artifact_Enumeration, Factor_Executable_Data_Values, Factor_Complete_Data_Quotation, RRA_Finite_Syntax_Construction, Natural_Binary_Digits | The executable complete data quotation. The nodes of the data syntax are numbered by a counter in construction order and addressed by the binary digits of their number (`compact_syntax_address`, injective, formed, the root keeps the empty address); the numbering is exactly an interval over the carrier (`data_syntax_carrier`, `data_syntax_address_injective`), and the finite constructors decode to the data syntax readdressed by it (`finite_data_syntax_sound`, `finite_data_syntax_exact`), so every returned artifact is a complete data quotation at the empty root (`finite_data_syntax_complete_quotation`). Target-bearing terms are rejected; malformed payload bytes are kept for the separate formation and admission checks. Before, children were addressed by prefixing, so an address grew with its depth and a quotation with the square of a data list. The executable complete data quotation is the data syntax readdressed. `data_syntax_position` numbers the nodes by a counter in construction order and `data_syntax_carrier` proves that the numbering is exactly an interval over the carrier, so it is injective; `compact_syntax_address` writes a number in binary digits (reusing `natural_binary_digits`; injective, formed, the root keeps the empty address). `finite_data_syntax_sound`/`_exact` state the result as `push_object` of the syntax by that map, and `finite_data_syntax_complete_quotation` keeps its statement, so every consumer of the quotation contract is unchanged. The interval argument applies to any syntax whose construction addresses children by prefixing: the native compiler's pattern, schema and definition syntax is the next instance. |

the step `finite_goal_successors` (interface and head unified with the goal by `finite_unify_pairs`, premises and material premises as goals; a material goal solved by R1's `finite_material_resolution`, one successor per solution W, each field unified with its instance under W, `finite_material_instance_pairs`); selection `finite_resolution_select` (ground, independent, solvable material, ground part; least socket path within a class, `'s::linorder`); the bounded search `finite_resolution_search` keeping every alternative (outcome: the fsets of successful states and diagnoses `resolution_diagnosis`: cut, stuck, refuted at a constructed value, refused certificate), ancestors pruned; ground certificates `finite_state_proofs` (residual variables at the empty payload, `finite_residual_term`); the per-call result `finite_program_resolution` (resolved with the certificates the finite proof checker accepts, refuted, unresolved with its diagnosis): `finite_program_resolution_accepted`, `finite_program_resolution_sound` (positive meaning through `finite_checks_schema_proof_exact` and `schema_proof_sound`, for every construction), `finite_program_resolution_refuted`; | Complete_Value_References | Main | Each reference recovers the complete supplied value; a first occurrence in a prefix stays where it was (`value_reference_index_append_member`). A sequence's table is the supplied one extended in first occurrence (`value_reference_sequence_table`), its indices are those of the final table (`value_reference_sequence_indices`), and the references of a concatenation are computed segment by segment against empty tables and merged (`value_reference_sequence_segment`, `value_reference_sequence_concat`, `value_reference_merged`, `value_reference_chunks`). The references of a mapped sequence, for any map, are the first-occurrence references of the sequence composed with those of its distinct values mapped (`value_reference_sequence_map`, over `value_reference_fold_map_first`): two values with one image meet in the second run. A list of references reads a list of values positionally (`value_reference_reads`): a concatenation part by part (`value_reference_reads_append`, `value_reference_reads_cons`), a sequence's references in its table (`value_reference_reads_sequence`), kept by every table that keeps the values read (`value_reference_reads_preserved`). |

| Factor_Demanded_Graph_Readings | Finite_Demanded_Closures, Factor_Recovered_Graph_Sharing | The second instance of the demanded reading of `Finite_Demanded_Closures`: a recovered proof graph reads a proof node only at the sites its root reaches through the premises the nodes discharge (successors: the children a node's premises cite; `finite_proof_site_reading`, `finite_proof_children`). The node grammar supplies the reading's boundary (`finite_proof_reading_position`: a node is read from a record at its use, so its site is a position of the environment); the universe formulation's sites are the traversal's (`finite_native_proof_sites_rooted`, through the converse of the discharge edges) and so are its rows (`finite_demanded_proof_readings`); the traversal is the code of `finite_recovered_graph`, with environment formation checked once. Replaying a judgment on a whole artifact therefore reads no node at the addresses of its literal target, whose square reading a node at every position cost: at 401, 801 and 1,601 addresses the demanded graph reading takes 0.011, 0.024 and 0.050 seconds, where reading at every position took 0.35, 2.5 and 19.6. |
| Factor_Finite_Accumulated_Data_Syntax | Factor_Finite_Data_Syntax, Factor_Finite_Syntax_Accumulation | The executable code of the complete data quotation: each row is constructed at its final compact address, threading the node counter, and accumulated once (`finite_data_syntax_rows_exact`, `finite_data_syntax_accumulated`); each row family is then listed once in its canonical order (`finite_data_syntax_accumulated_code`), so later listings, comparisons and formation checks recognize the listing in one pass instead of sorting it again. Every optional result is unchanged. The prefix construction had emitted its rows in ascending order by accident of its addresses; compact rows arrive in construction order, and without the listing every later listing, comparison and formation check of a quotation sorted it again (at 4,000 rows, twenty listings 0.446 against 0.027 seconds, twenty equalities 0.849 against 0.061). Canonical order is a property the consumers' fast paths read (`Finite_Sorted_Set_Execution`), so a producer of finite sets states it where it constructs them. |
| Factor_Finite_Syntax_Accumulation | Factor_Finite_Artifact_Enumeration, RRA_Finite_Syntax_Construction, HOL-Library.List_Lexorder, HOL-Library.Product_Lexorder | Complete syntax rows accumulated once: payload rows and pair rows stated by the node's root, two ports and the roots of its two children, so every addressing of the syntax accumulates through the same rows; their objects are the original syntax join and attachment. Rows listed again in their canonical order present the same object (`finite_syntax_rows_object_listed`). Pair rows stated by a node's root, two ports and two child roots (`finite_syntax_rows_pair`), so accumulated rows serve every addressing of the syntax; the prefix-specific pair accumulation was removed as unused. |

The requests lie in the term demand whenever the entry is a definition (`workflow_stage_requests_demanded`), so the stage is available wherever the earlier one was (`workflow_stage_demand_ready`); the stage's exactness theorem, its reference and the meaning of its evidence keep their statements, so every client consumed them unchanged, and the keyed code equations restate the same demand. |

| Native_Development_Execution | Native_Development_Execution_Base, Native_Execution_Refinements, Development_Presentation | Parallel complete-question execution and native export of the closed development cycle and its producer comparison. |
| Native_Development_Steering | Factor_Steered_Execution_Sharing, Native_Development_Execution, Native_Execution_Refinements, Development_Presentation | Native export of computed producer selection, complete original subjects and subsequent request execution. |
| Native_Workflow_Execution | Native_Workflow_Execution_Base, Native_Execution_Refinements, Workflow_Presentation | Native export of original requirements, complete workflow branches, selected input scope and parallel requests. |

## Native source development

| Theory | Imports | Responsibility |
|---|---|---|
| Factor_Development_Criterion_Sources | Factor_Development_Cases, Factor_Finite_Ground_Source, Factor_Finite_System_Unions | Reusable construction of native criteria with exact original source meaning: the ground condition of a finite family is a program condition (`finite_ground_condition_program`); a program of its own is installed together with the guard source (`finite_standalone_condition`, `_total`, `_exact`: its entry keeps its own meaning). |
| Native_Collection_Programs | Factor_Development_Criterion_Sources, Factor_Finite_Payload_Literals, Factor_Finite_System_Fields | Native definitions over native coordinates: ordinary rules whose conclusion binds every variable their premises use (`finite_native_rule`, read back by `finite_native_rule_eq_iff`), and the n-ary tuple of a conclusion's variables (`term_tuple`, its pattern `finite_pattern_tuple`, `evaluate_pattern_tuple`). A family of rules at one site with its unfolding and step proved once (`native_rule_family`: `holds_clause`, `holds_cases`, `step`, `native_step`). The family has one law (`native_rule_law`, introduced by `native_rule_lawI` from the family and its rules being native). At any support relation `Y` it gives `rule_instance`, `supported_clause` and `read_clause`: a reading of every rule at a clause's evaluated conclusion is a reading of the clause. At the positive meaning it gives `holds_rule` (the native rule a holding call applied), `step_at` (one rule, formation on the conclusion variables no premise reads), `step_rule` (the same with formation on the whole conclusion, no set difference) and `exact`. The listed form: a family written as the rules of a listing of triples `(c,p,ps)` (`native_rule_listing`, `native_rule_listing_member`) has the law as `native_listed_law`. The rule a clause or a call applied is read back to its triple once (`rule_triple`, the one use of the constructor's injectivity), so no use decodes a rule: at the positive meaning `holds_triple` returns the triple a holding call applied, `step_triple` takes a listed triple with formation on its conclusion, and `exact` is over the listed triples; at any support relation `Y`, `supported_triple` returns the triple of a clause whose premises are supported, and `read_triple` reads a clause through every listed triple at its evaluated conclusion, which least-fixed-point arguments over composed programs consume with each program's own `unfold_rule`, stated at a listed triple. Programs taking the listed law, each at its family's listing stated once and proved to present the family's rules (sublocale `triples`, its law read as `triples.law`; `native_member_listing` with `native_member_rules_listing`, and likewise `native_every_listing`, `native_some_listing`, `native_keyed_search_listing`; each program's `unfold_rule` at membership in its listing): the collection notions membership (`native_member_program.exact`), every element in a context (`native_every_program`, presenting the shared `context_list_rule_relation` by `relation_equation`), some element (`native_some_program.exact`, whose rest rule is membership's later rule `native_member_later` at the traversal's own site) and the value a keyed table holds (`native_keyed_search_program.exact`); `Native_Path_Stores`' `native_store_search_program` and `native_store_absent_program`, which keep their sublocale `law` beside `triples`; and the one-rule programs below. A program is the families at its sites (`finite_rule_program`), and a formed one with distinct sites is a `native_rule_family` at each (`finite_rule_program_family`). A sublist of a formed rule program's definitions with distinct sites keeps, at its sites, the meaning its own formed program gives them (`finite_rule_program_join`, from the locality contract `positive_meaning_shared_definitions` through `finite_rule_program_agrees`): every join of rule programs consumes it. One rule, every premise at the conclusion's evaluation (`native_conjunction_program`, sublocale `triples`): a site whose family is one rule `finite_native_rule p ps`, every premise's variables bound by `p`, holds of a term exactly when some evaluation formed on `p`'s variables evaluates `p` to it with every premise holding (`exact`), and at every evaluation `f` exactly when `f` is formed where no premise reads and every premise holds at `f` (`at`). A rule without premise is its case of the empty list. A single premise on a rearrangement of the conclusion's variables (`native_rearranging_program`, sublocale `triples`; `supported`: at a clause of its site whose premise lies in any support relation `Y`, the clause's conclusion and its premise's call in `Y`) is its case of one premise: sublocale `conjunction`, `exact` and `at` read from it. Its named instances: the swap (`native_swap_rule`, `native_swap_program.exact`: the site holds of a pair exactly when the callee holds of it exchanged, so a traversal that calls its element beside its context reaches a callee that reads the element first), membership with the list first (`list_cited_program`, membership's program behind the swap; `list_exact`: `(w,Pair_Term (data_list_term xs) x)` holds exactly when `x∈set xs` and every member is formed), and the call that passes a pair's first component along as context (`native_context_call_rule`, `native_context_call_program.exact`: `Pair x y` exactly when the callee holds of `Pair x (Pair y x)`). Rule-program relocation and formation (moved from `Development_Package_Program`, task 426): `relocated_definitions` (a definition list moved by a map of sites) with `relocated_rule_program` (its program is `finite_rename_system` of the original), `relocated_formed`, `relocated_fixed` (a map fixing the sites of a formed program fixes it), `relocated_sites`, `relocated_definitions_sites`, `relocated_append`, `relocated_drop`, `finite_rename_schema_fixed`; `finite_rule_program_formed_parts` (a list with distinct sites each of whose definitions lies in a formed part it holds is formed: the rule-program form of `system_union_agree_formed`); `finite_rule_program_set`, `finite_rule_program_append_payloads`, `relocated_payloads`. |
| Native_Path_Stores | Native_Collection_Programs, Binary_Path_Stores, Finite_Presented_Collections | Keys and tables as structure: a bit is a shape (the empty payload or a pair of empty payloads), a path is the data list of its bits (`path_term`, `path_term_injective`), a list of paths the data list of the paths (`keys_term`, with `keys_term_formed`, `keys_term_pair`, `keys_term_Cons`, `keys_term_eq_iff`, `keys_term_inj`: one notion for a row's citation lists and a request body's families, moved here from `Development_Verdict_Statements` by task 295, which retired `Development_Rows`' `development_row_family`), and a path store is the existing binary store presented as structure (`store_term`: the empty store a leaf, a node its optional value beside its two children). The native search of a store along a key, in a context (`native_store_search_rules`: found at the empty path, left on the one bit, right on the other), its site's meaning taken from the family's listed law (`native_store_search_program.triples`, the `native_listed_law` at its listing `native_store_search_listing`, which presents its rules by `native_store_search_rules_listing`, beside `law`) and its rules read once at a listed triple and any support relation (`unfold_rule`, which a consumer composes with `triples.read_triple`, as readiness's and the reach's invariants do), with its contract for every key (`native_store_search_program.sound`, `.exact`: a search holds exactly when the key is a path at which the store holds a value checked in the context; `exact_held`: the same for one store whose values are formed where it holds them, `⋀bs y. store_lookup S bs=Some y ⟹ term_formed (val y)`, derived from `exact` through `store_term_cong`; `held_at`: at a key where the store holds `v`, the search holds exactly when the check holds of `v`); any native definition that looks a value up by a key instantiates it, and the keyed walk stays for tables whose keys are not paths. The path store of a list of rows (`path_store`; `path_store_found`: a value found is a row's; `path_store_lookup`: exact on single-valued rows). A key is present in a store built from rows exactly when it is a key of those rows, whatever the rows (`path_store_present`, from `path_store_fold_present`; no single-valuedness is needed), which moved here from `Development_Verdict_Mentions` beside the store it is about. The other half of the search, store absence: a path at which a store holds nothing, descending the store by the path's bits' shapes to the empty store or to a node whose optional value is absent (`native_store_absent_rules`, six rules at one site: the empty path at the empty store and at a valueless node, and each bit at the empty store and at a node; its listed law `native_store_absent_program.triples` at its listing `native_store_absent_listing`, `native_store_absent_rules_listing`), with its contract for every key term (`native_store_absent_program.exact`: absence holds exactly when the key is a path at which the store's lookup is `None`; `exact_at_path`, the form its consumers read). An empty result and a failed one stay apart, positively: where the store holds a value at a key, even an empty one, absence fails (`absent_not_at_value`), a key that is no path has no absence (`absent_refuses_unpresented`) as it has no search, and the search and absence never hold of one key and store (`store_search_absent_exclusive`) — exclusion, neither program being the other's negation. Executable paths and stores with their readers (`finite_path`, `finite_path_bits`, `finite_store`, `decode_finite_store`), their injectivity and formation (`finite_store_option_injective`, `finite_store_injective`, `finite_path_formed`, `finite_store_option_formed`, `finite_store_formed`). Every path store is canonical (`path_store_canonical`), so two single-valued listings of one set of rows build the same store whatever their order and repetitions (`path_store_rows`). The one row and table presentation: a row as its path with its value (`finite_store_row`) and a table as the store of its listing (`finite_listing_store`), with injectivity, formation and decoding; stores with equal presentations agree at every path up to the value presentation (`finite_store_lookup_agree`), and over single-valued listings whose values are presented injectively on the values present (`inj_on`), a table's presentation is exact on the rows it holds (`finite_listing_store_identifies`, `finite_listing_store_exact`): a table's word is a function of its rows, not of the listing chosen. A table of presented rows is the store of the rows read back (`finite_row_read`, `finite_rows_table`), equal to the store of every single-valued listing of them (`finite_rows_table_listing`) and identifying them (`finite_rows_table_identifies`), moved here from `Development_Row_Data` (task 354). A store's presentation reads only the values it holds (`store_term_cong`), and a row presenter is injective on the rows whose values it presents injectively (`finite_store_row_injective_on`, of which `finite_store_row_injective` is the case of every value). The readiness table and the development tables are its instances. |
| Native_Path_Store_Indexes | Carrier_Indexes, Native_Path_Stores | The path store is the native carrier of the index notion. Host part: `path_store_carrier_index` is `carrier_index` with rows over paths, formation `single_valued (set rows)`, queries `UNIV`, key `id`, index `path_store`, search `store_lookup … = Some v`, discharged by `path_store_lookup`. Native part, in the context of `native_store_search_program` (not a sublocale, since it is conditional on the values' formation): `index` is `native_carrier_index` of that host part with site `(k,Pair_Term x (Pair_Term t j))∈positive_meaning P`, context formation `term_formed`, keys presented by `path_term` (distinguishing by `path_term_injective`), stores by `store_term val`, checker the program's `ch` at `val v`, under `⋀y. term_formed (val y)` (from `exact`, since the instance is claimed for every store); `index_sound` is `index_search_sound` of the same, for every `val` (from `sound`). A use whose values are formed only where one store holds them reads that store through `Native_Path_Stores`' `exact_held`, not `index`; its host lookups take `path_store_carrier_index`'s `query_search`, as `Development_Rows` and `Development_Located_Rows` do. Reuse: an interpretation `X` of `native_store_search_program` made in a theory importing this one inherits `X.index` and `X.index_sound`, as `readiness_settled_searches` and `reach_searches` do (their theories import this one since task 74); then `query_search`, `site_query` and the notion's other laws at the program. The restriction law: `path_store_restrict` (the path store of any listing filtered to a key set has the full store's lookup at every kept key, from `path_store_fold_restrict` over the replacements; no single-valuedness) and `path_store_restrict_outside` (nothing at a key outside the set, with no premise); `lookup_eq_by_some` (two lookups finding the same values are equal). A theory importing this one and `Binary_Store_Indexes` names `Native_Path_Store_Indexes.path_store_carrier_index`, the short name resolving to the identity-keyed store's. |
| Factor_Finite_Development_Questions | Factor_Development_Criterion_Sources, Finite_Binary_Values, Native_Collection_Programs | A question has a subject and a scope: the scope is a native program holding of any subject paired with each candidate (`finite_scope_rule`: its premise is the guard's universal recognizer, so the scope never restates the subject; `finite_scope_program_meaning`, `finite_development_source_meaning`), and a question of a subject judges each candidate by its conditions on the subject (`finite_subject_question`, `finite_subject_question_conditions`). A candidate's index is the binary presentation of its natural (`finite_development_index`), one payload whose size grows with the number of digits. The unary natural data term it replaced grew the source quadratically and made the scope review's input ten times larger; the question reads only the equality of indices (`finite_development_index_eq`), which the presentation's injectivity supplies. Reuse (REASONING_REUSE.md, "A finite value family as a ground program, which asserts nothing else"): The questions over finite value families are the question of the empty subject. |

| Finite_Demanded_Closures | Bootstrap_Finite_Closure, Finite_Set_Composition, HOL-Library.While_Combinator | A rooted reading of rows at sites. The universe formulation reads every site of a universe and keeps the rows at the sites the roots reach through the edges the rows supply (`finite_rooted_sites`); the frontier traversal reads each demanded site once, only after a root has reached it (`finite_demanded_readings`). Under the reading's own boundary, that every site with a row lies in the universe, both return the same sites and the same rows there (`finite_demanded_readings_exact`). The traversal is correct without a universe whenever it returns, having read a closed set of sites (`finite_demanded_readings_closed`), and a closed universe containing the roots bounds it (`finite_demanded_readings_within`, `_exact`). The traversal of the sites alone (`finite_demanded_sites`) is the traversal's projection onto its sites and frontier and returns the same sites without keeping the rows (`finite_demanded_sites_readings`, through `while_option_commute_invariant`: a projection that commutes with the step commutes with the loop), so a reading that needs only the reached sites instantiates it and keeps no rows. The universe, the reading and the successors are the caller's; nothing here judges what a row means. | Linear_Comparisons | HOL-Library.List_Lexorder, HOL-Library.Product_Lexorder | A comparison with three outcomes equals the linear order and composes through listings, pairs, naturals and addresses. |
| Prefix_Key_Comparisons | Linear_Comparisons | The notion of DECISIONS.md "A structural comparison computes a prefix key's order; which component it meets first is its view's", checked. A view presents a value as a head and a list of children in the order the view meets them; the key is a parameter with its one-level equation. Locale `prefix_key` (fixes `head`, `children`, `key`; assumes `key_node`, `head_arity`, `node_determined`): `key_nonempty`, `child_key_shorter`, `key_prefix` (prefix-free), `key_injective`. Locale `prefix_key_comparison` (adds `compare`, assumes `compare_node`: the heads' `compare_linear`, then `compare_listed` over the children): `compare_keys` (keys continued by anything compare as their values, the continuations only where the values are equal), `compare_order`, `compare_equal`, `key_less_eq`, `key_less`. The lemmas over lists of children the inductions use, over any key: `listed_key_prefix`, `listed_key_compare`. A use interprets both locales and proves only its view's one-level equations; no component order is fixed and nothing of cost is claimed. No simp, intro or code attributes. |
| Formed_Call_Closures | Keyed_Native_Evaluation, Factor_Constructed_Program_Applications | The closure of formed requests reads constructed applications: a check made where its premise is established (`Established_Premises`). A premise call of an application of a formed program is formed (`finite_program_call_premise_formed`), so once the requests are checked formed the closure reads `finite_constructed_applications` at every call and returns the same calls (`keyed_call_closure_formed`), the arguments' formation walked once instead of at every demanded call. The keyed closure at the call key is the constant `native_call_closure`, which contains its requests (`native_call_closure_requests`, moved from `Criticism_Samples` by task 483), which every use reads (`keyed_call_closure_native`, a code unfolding) and whose code equation checks the program and the requests at its entry: the closure is an instance of `Established_Premises`' `checked_premise` (`native_call_closure_checked_premise`) and its code equation is that notion's `checked_at_entry` there (`native_call_closure_code`); `Positioned_Native_Evaluation` imports it, so every evaluation and export at the call key computes it. The traversal of demanded sites depends on its reading only where it reaches: two readings agreeing on a condition the roots satisfy and every successor keeps return the same sites (`finite_demanded_sites_cong`, stated here at its first use; its notion is `Finite_Demanded_Closures`'). Observed on the verdict's judgment (task 290): the machinery state's demand, 13,364 calls, 30.9 s to 25.2 s; the seeded state's, 2,985 calls, 0.92 s to 0.66 s. |
| Ordered_Artifact_Comparison | Ordered_Complete_Artifacts, Linear_Comparisons | Complete artifacts compare field by field over their canonical listings in one pass; listed rows with their field sizes are ordered keys. A target's key is its artifact's compared rows with its occurrence (`finite_target_key`, `finite_target_occurrence`), injective (`finite_target_key_injective`). |
| Ordered_Term_Comparison | Ordered_Finite_Terms, Ordered_Artifact_Comparison | Executable terms compare structurally and stop at the first differing subterm, exactly in the order of their prefix keys. `finite_term_compare` is the prefix-key comparison of the plain view (`finite_term_comparison: prefix_key_comparison`, its one-level equation `finite_term_compare_node` proved by cases on both terms); `finite_term_compare_keys`, `finite_term_compare_order` and the three structural code equations of `ordered_factor_term` are its `compare_keys`, `compare_order`, `key_less_eq`, `key_less` and `compare_equal`. Also `compare_linear_option`, `compare_linear_ordered_artifact`, `finite_term_tag`, `finite_term_key_tag`, `finite_term_compare_distinct_tags`. |
| Right_Ordered_Terms | Ordered_Term_Comparison | Terms are ordered at the right component of a pair first: the prefix-key comparison (`Prefix_Key_Comparisons`) at the view that lists a pair's components right first, the reverse of the plain view's children, its key the key of the mirrored term (`finite_term_right_comparison: prefix_key_comparison`; `mirror_term`, an involution, exchanges the components of every pair). `finite_term_compare_right` computes the order on the term itself; `finite_term_compare_right_mirror`, the instance's `compare_order` with the plain one, equates it with the plain comparison of mirrored terms; the three code equations of `right_ordered_term` are the instance's `key_less_eq`, `key_less` and `compare_equal`, so equality is decided by the same comparison. Keys of calls ordered so tell two calls of one context apart where they differ. Reuse (REASONING_REUSE.md, "A structural comparison computes a prefix key's order, meeting components in its view's order"): keys over terms whose left components are shared use it. It rests on the convention of the programs building the terms (a notion passes its context on the left), not on a property of terms, so it is chosen per key and never as the equality of all terms: decided globally, it made the comparison of presented generations, which share their right components, 15 percent slower. |
| Keyed_Value_References | Complete_Value_References, HOL-Library.RBT, Linear_Comparisons | First-occurrence reference indices are found through an ordered tree of injective keys with one three-outcome comparison per node; indices and table are unchanged. |
| Shared_Term_Tables | Keyed_Value_References, Tree_Map_Indexes, Ordered_Finite_Terms, Keyed_Demanded_Sites, Prefix_Key_Comparisons | The notion of DECISIONS.md "An evaluation's calls are built over the shared subterms of its requests", checked. Leaves `factor_leaf` (`leaf_term`), shapes `shape` (`Leaf_Shape`, `Pair_Shape` of two references) keyed injectively by `shape_key` (a target leaf by `Ordered_Finite_Terms`' order). The table: `share_term`/`share_terms` (`value_reference_step` at every node), `shared_table`, formed `table_formed` (distinct, a pair's components at smaller positions), decoding `reference_term`; its build `keyed_shared_family` (`keyed_shared_family_exact`, code of `shared_table`): the keyed first-occurrence step, stated once for any value and injective key (`keyed_reference_step`, `keyed_reference_state`, `keyed_reference_step_exact`; its insertion `keyed_reference_insert`, the index notion's update `tree_map_updates` at the first-occurrence table; its fold over a list `keyed_reference_sequence`, `keyed_reference_sequence_exact`, with a state's table `keyed_reference_state_table` and the empty table's state `keyed_reference_state_empty`), taken at `shape_key` (`keyed_share_shape`) and threaded through the terms; its index of shapes `shape_index`, a carrier index through `tree_map_carrier_index` (`shape_carrier_index`, `shape_index_lookup`, `keyed_shared_family_index`). Shared terms `shared_term`, `shared_decode`, `shared_canonical`; constructors `shared_leaf`, `shared_pair` over any search (`table_find`, `keyed_shared_family_find`); the canonical form of a term, the constructors folded over it (`shared_form`), canonical and decoding to the term (`shared_form_exact`) and the only canonical shared term that does (`shared_form_canonical`); view `shared_view` (`term_view`). Contract: (1) `share_term_exact`, `share_terms_exact`, `shared_table_presents`, `share_term_preserves`, `share_terms_preserves`; (2) `shared_canonical_equality`; (3) `shared_leaf_exact`, `shared_pair_exact`, `shared_canonical_components`; (4) `shared_view_decode`, `shared_view_canonical`. Order: `shared_term` is `linorder` by an injective prefix key, the notion of `Prefix_Key_Comparisons` at the view meeting a pair's left component first (`shared_children`; `shared_term_view`, interpretations `shared_term_prefix_key` and `shared_term_comparison`, its `compare_node` `compare_shared_node`), which gives `shared_term_key_prefix`, `shared_term_key_injective` and `compare_shared_terms_keys`; executed by `compare_shared_terms` (`compare_shared_terms_linear`, code of `<` and `≤`), a reference compared as a number (`compare_shared_references`, `shared_reference_less`); the key's contract `shared_call_demanded_sites`. No reference is a subject; nothing of cost is claimed. |
| Factor_Policy_Scope_Sharing | Factor_Certified_Policy_Readings | A policy cause reads its generation judgment scopes once and supplies them to both original existential checks. |
| Factor_Formation_Once_Definitions | Factor_Formation_Once_Readings, Factor_Executable_Judgment_Retention | Definition, schema, scoped pattern, premise, material, call and vector readings consume formation-free bodies, so each guarded entry checks environment formation once. Each guarded entry (family, call, pattern record, pattern vector, schema, scoped pattern and definition readings, and package formation) is a `checked_premise` instance, `*_checked_premise`, whose exactness is its `*_formed_exact` fact and whose code equation is `checked_through` at the statement's full arity; seven of these entries are roots the seeded state expands, so their restatement at the premise's arity is a refinement of seeded constants, landing as its own task with the seed's words re-recorded, while package formation's changes no word (DECISIONS.md "What a refinement keeps equal when a recorded state presents the refined constant's code equations"); definition rows (`finite_native_definition_rows_formed_definitions_code`) hoist the definition reading's check by `checked_through` and `checked_union`. `formed_environment_object` is the establishing fact for objects. A package's demanded slots (task 485): the formed bodies of the slot readers — `finite_family_endpoints_formed`, `finite_native_premise_family_slots_formed`, `finite_native_schema_slots_formed`, `finite_native_definition_slots_formed`, each exact under a formed environment (`finite_family_endpoints_formed_exact`, `finite_native_premise_readings_formed_exact`, `finite_native_definition_slots_formed_exact`), a definition's slots empty outside it (`finite_native_definition_slots_unformed`) — make `finite_native_package_demands` a `checked_premise` instance (`finite_native_package_demands_checked_premise`, refusal the root requests' slots) whose code equation `finite_native_package_demands_formed_once_code` checks the environment's formation once for the whole package. The demands over a supplied family of sites (`finite_native_package_demands_over`, equal to the demands at the package's own sites, `finite_native_package_demands_over_sites`) let the package and judgment environments read the sites once for their sources and their demands (`finite_native_package_environment_shared_code`, `finite_native_judgment_environment_shared_code`, HOL's `Let`, task 494). An application's demands are a `checked_premise` instance too (`finite_native_application_demands_checked_premise`, refusal empty, code `finite_native_application_demands_formed_once_code`), over the call readings' formed body and the term readings' (`finite_term_readings_carrier_formed`, `finite_term_readings_carrier_formed_exact`). |
| Factor_Complete_Data_Walks | Factor_Finite_Prepared_Data_Readings, RRA_Executable_Records, Finite_Sorted_Set_Execution, HOL-Library.List_Lexorder, HOL-Library.Product_Lexorder | A formed data reading follows one walk over the unique leaf or two-field record of each node; a complete reading compares the walk's sorted listing with the canonical carrier once instead of joining interiors at every record. |
| Finite_Ordered_Set_Difference | Finite_Sorted_Set_Execution, RRA_Finite_Role_Projections, HOL-Library.List_Lexorder | The keyed merge, stated once: `keyed_difference f` subtracts two listings ordered by a key in one merge pass comparing the heads' keys, with its contract `keyed_difference_filter` (on listings sorted by a key injective on their members it is the filter of the first by absence from the second) and `keyed_difference_subset`; `keyed_difference_paired` (with `insort_key_paired`, `sort_key_paired`, `keyed_difference_pairs`): the merge of two `sort_key f` listings equals the merge on the listings paired with their keys once, sorted and merged by `fst`, so a code equation computes each key once. `ascending_difference` is its identity-key instance (`ascending_difference=keyed_difference id`), its recursive equations its code equations (`ascending_difference_simps`), with `ascending_difference_filter` and `ascending_difference_listing` (the canonical listing of a finite set difference); `finite_unreferenced_positions_listing_code` lists the unreferenced positions of a structure by two such passes. |
| Factor_Ordered_Generation_Checking | RRA_Generation_Child_Row_Images, Factor_Ordered_Target_Equality | Generation checks and readiness execute their original equations, comparing targets and predecessors as values instead of encoding them as complete words at every node. |
| Factor_Finite_Source_Construction_Sharing | Factor_Finite_Source_Construction | A source constructor reads its native source once: `finite_construct_source_read_once` passes the program `finite_native_source` returns to the extension context's remaining checks and to the installation, which read the same source again, so every compiled requirement, condition and scope critic reads its condition's source once; the constructor, its result and its contract are unchanged. |

| Ordered_Member_Trees | Tree_Map_Indexes, HOL-Library.FSet | Exact last-occurrence deduplication and finite-set membership through an ordered index built once from the canonical listing; the deduplication's recorded rows take the insertion's lookup from `tree_map_updates` (`ordered_remdups_fold`); a listed set, a list read as the set of its members, is indexed the same way (`ordered_member_tree_listed`). Shared by investigations and keyed native evaluation. Reuse (REASONING_REUSE.md, "An index of a carrier by a key"): The ordered member index and last-occurrence deduplication, factored out of `Ordered_Finite_Rows` at their second use; investigations and keyed evaluation now instantiate the one index. |
| Member_Tree_Indexes | Carrier_Indexes, Ordered_Member_Trees, Keyed_Finite_Sets | The instances of the red-black tree's index (`Tree_Map_Indexes`). `member_tree_index` (a finite set of a linear order, value `unit`, index `ordered_member_tree`) is the tree's instance read through the canonical listing by `carrier_index_through_key`, and `member_tree_updates` the tree's update at unit values; `listed_member_tree_index` (a list through `fset_of_list`) and locale `keyed_set_index` (a key with a left inverse, index the tree of the key image) derive from it. Query forms the uses cite: `member_tree_lookup`, `member_tree_found`, `member_tree_absent`, `member_tree_insert`, `listed_member_lookup`, `keyed_set_index.member_query` and `keyed_set_index.members_subset`; the lookup forms are the notion's `lookup_found` and `lookup_queries_found` at unit values. Abbreviation `member_holds`. |
| Tree_Map_Indexes | Carrier_Indexes, HOL-Library.RBT | The index notion interpreted in the red-black tree, below every theory that indexes by it. `tree_map_index` and `tree_map_updates`: a list of rows with distinct keys, values of any type, key the identity, index `RBT.bulkload`, search `RBT.lookup T k=Some v` (abbreviation `tree_search`, from `RBT.lookup_bulkload`), update `RBT.insert` (from `RBT.lookup_insert`). Moved from `Member_Tree_Indexes` (task 115) so that `Ordered_Member_Trees` cites the insertion's lookup. |
| First_Index_Trees | Tree_Map_Indexes, Complete_Value_References, HOL-Library.Multiset | A list indexed by the first positions of its members (task 249, moved from `Development_State_Edit`): `first_index_tree key xs` (the bulkload of the keys zipped with their positions) with `first_index_tree_lookup` (its lookup at `key x` is `value_reference_index x xs`), `first_index_tree_absent`, `first_index_tree_found`, `map_of_first_index`, `first_index_image`; `first_index_carrier_index` (the tree map's index read through the key: the through instance of `carrier_index_through_key` carried by `Carrier_Indexes.carrier_index_search_agrees`); `value_reference_index_append_absent` (the sibling of `value_reference_index_append_member`); a distinct list is recovered from a distinct listing of the members it keeps by sorting on their first positions: `value_reference_index_nth`, `first_positions_filter_sorted`, `first_positions_sort`. |
| Merge_Sort_Keys | HOL-Library.Sorting_Algorithms | `sort_key` is the library's merge sort at a key's comparator (task 269): `insort_key_comparator` (`Sorting_Algorithms.insort (key f default)=insort_key f`), `sort_key_by_mergesort` (`sort_key f xs=Sorting_Algorithms.mergesort (key f default) xs`), so a use states `sort_key` and computes the merge sort (code `mergesort_code`). Hides the library's short names `sort`, `sorted`, `insort` and `Comparator.key`, which would shadow the list theory's and the development's variables. |
| Ordered_Finite_Rows | Finite_Investigation_Interface, Member_Tree_Indexes, HOL-Library.Product_Lexorder | Re-cited: membership through `member_tree_lookup`, the index notion's instance. Investigation rows are selected through the ordered member index of a complete set, in the last-occurrence order of the original list. |
| Projected_Identity_Execution | Main | A cheap actual observation may reject identity; the injective complete view decides it. |
| RRA_Linked_Record_Candidates | RRA_Finite_Syntax_Bodies, Established_Premises, Candidate_Generators | Record candidates follow actual successor incidences, and object formation is checked once per read. The linked rows generate the accepted record candidates (`finite_linked_record_rows_candidates`, through `candidate_generator.accepted_generated`); formation is the premise of `finite_record_candidates` checked at its entry (`finite_record_candidates_checked_premise`), so the code equation `finite_record_candidates_formed_once_code` is `checked_at_entry` at the object's arity, the arity condition remaining in the guard of `finite_record_body_candidates`. |


## Export content boundaries

Content that export theories previously held now lives in a base theory without the
shared refinement import, so refinement changes rebuild only thin export theories.

| Isabelle_Constant_Closure | Main | The shared traversal of a checked Isabelle context: the kernel definitions of a constant, the code equations in effect for it, and the closure of constants reached through the items each contributes. Exporters choose the items; an answer frame notes the code equations in effect as its fact through the same reading, which takes the declared equations as every function transformer receives them, untransformed and unrewritten; the traversal establishes no semantic claim. The code equations in effect for a constant, read exactly as declared, moved here from the exporter so the exporter and every answer frame read them in one way. Under the global context the certificate had been rewritten by every simplification rule; an answer's own equation could be read as the incumbent. Certified alone (empty simpset, no function transformers) the reading silently returned nothing for Suc-pattern equations under Code_Target_Nat; the code graph's certificate is not local (callees' sort demands). The declared equations are now taken from the list `Code.get_cert` hands every function transformer, untransformed, unoverloaded as certification does. |
| Map_Filter_Lists | Main | The generic facts of a list read by an optional reading (`List.map_filter`), stated once for every theory that reads a list so: membership is the optional image (`map_filter_member`), the result depends only on the reading at the members (`map_filter_agree`), distinct results take each value at one member (`map_filter_unique`), and among distinct members that is exactly distinctness of the results (`map_filter_distinct_iff`). Moved here from `Development_Constant_Problems`, `Isabelle_Local_Names` and `Isabelle_Entities`, below all their uses; `map_filter_unique` now cites `map_filter_member` instead of proving its direction again. |
| Isabelle_Terms | Finite_Presented_Coordinates | Checked Pure types and terms as structural subjects, with abstractions keeping no bound name. A term is stated over what presents its types (`'ty isabelle_term_with`; the kernel's term `isabelle_term` carries a type at every occurrence), so a term over positions of a table of types is the same datatype, read back by its own map. Name, sort, type and term presentations compose the generic payload, sequence and pair presentations and are injective members of their notions’ classes. A term over positions of a table of types is the same datatype, and reading it back is the datatype's own map, so no second term notion or translation is introduced. |
| Isabelle_Entities | Isabelle_Terms, Finite_Inference_Development, Map_Filter_Lists | Entities of a checked context: base and development constant declarations, kernel definitions, non-definitional specifications and code equations in effect. Constants, equation heads and subjects are read structurally; reachability from roots is the least closure of ordinary finite inference rules, and undeclared, malformed and unreached entities are computed observations. A rooted state is presented as its ordered roots with its context, once, for every report that presents one. A declaration's term (the constant with its declared type) is read separately from the constant it declares. The exporter's obligation `isabelle_declared_once` (no two entities of a state declare one constant; owner: the exporter, which exports answer states too) is stated here once, beside `isabelle_declared_constant`; its uses discharge it. A condition sufficient for it: distinct declared constants (`isabelle_declared_once_distinct`, through `Map_Filter_Lists.map_filter_unique`). Its code equation (`isabelle_declared_once_code`, task 227): the constants declared by the state's declarations, repeated declarations removed, are distinct; proved through `map_filter_distinct_iff`. The obligation at every exported state is the exporter's theorem `NAME_declared_once` (`Isabelle_Entity_Export`, task 256), kernel-checked at the seeded and machinery states. An entity is stated over its term (`'t isabelle_entity_with`), as a term is over its types. One presentation of a rooted state (ordered roots and context), reused by the loop presentation and the syntax judgments instead of composing it inline. `development_declared` (task 249: the reading `isabelle_development_constants` uses, `development_constants_declared`) and `development_constants_member` (a constant is a development constant of a list exactly when some entity of it declares it so). |
| Isabelle_Readers | Isabelle_Entities, Finite_Presentation_Readers | Exact readers of the presentations of names, positions, sorts, types, terms, entities and contexts (`isabelle_entity_reads`, `isabelle_context_reads`): a presented entity arriving from anywhere is read as the entity it presents or refused as a whole. Constructors are identified by the payload their presentation begins with, read as a value and compared. Reuse (REASONING_REUSE.md, "Read a presentation back exactly"): Exact readers of names, positions, sorts, types, terms, entities and contexts; a constructor is identified by the payload its presentation begins with, read as a value. |
| Isabelle_Type_Tables | Isabelle_Entities, HOL-Library.RBT | A definition holds each distinct type of its terms once: a table of type nodes whose arguments are positions of earlier nodes, read in order into an ordered index (`isabelle_type_table`) and exact at every position of an ordered table (`isabelle_type_table_exact`); a state or terms presented over positions of that table are read back by the entity's and the term's own maps (`isabelle_shared_context`, `isabelle_shared_terms`), so every occurrence of a type reads one value. Which table presents a state is the exporter's transport, as its name table is. |
| Isabelle_Acceptance | Isabelle_Renaming, Factor_Finite_Ground_Evaluation | Acceptance proved once for the notion: the presented entities of a checked context are the ground clauses of one native program whose entry holds exactly of their presentations, so its known calls are exactly the accepted entities; native evaluation decides demanded entities exactly and always answers. The decision is total and equals the demanded entities split by membership in the supplied ones (`isabelle_demand_acceptance_members`, derived from the notion's exact theorem and the ground-source totality); it is the notion's code equation, so every use consumes the contract instead of installing, compiling and reading back a program to decide membership again. |
| Isabelle_Entity_Export | Isabelle_Type_Tables, Isabelle_Constant_Closure | The exporter inside a checked context: it follows the shared closure, translates types and terms constructor by constructor, and defines the entities of a development state, its roots and the roots of each named group as ordinary definitions of that context, all against one name table and each holding every distinct type once in a table of type nodes (the kernel repeats a type at every occurrence). The same roots can be exported again in a later context that extends the defining one; the constants a given theory introduces are then expanded and seeded too, so an answer state holds their specifications, and an introduced constant no root reaches is an unreached entity rather than an invisible one. An answer's introduced constants are the logical constants its theory declares; an abbreviation declares none (pending edit, parked in `.build/impl4/exporter-abbreviations.txt` until the adoption check completes). Every exported state, its roots and its groups are defined through a table holding each distinct type once; a term over any presentation of its types is translated by one function (`term_with`), of which the kernel's form (`term_term`) and the table's form are instances. The context construction takes further terms for its name table, so a caller presenting propositions against the same table (`Native_Control_Syntax_Statements`) reuses it instead of building a second context. A constant belongs to the fixed base when the session of the theory that declared it is a base session (`declaring_session`): a theory's long name is qualified by its session except the theory Pure's, whose long name is its session, so reading the qualifier alone had made every constant of Pure a development constant. Wherever it defines a state (`define`, `define_again`, through `define_state`), the exporter notes `NAME_declared_once`: `isabelle_declared_once NAME_context`, the obligation of `Isabelle_Entities`, kernel-checked with no evaluation oracle. The proof reads only the declarations: `isabelle_shared_declared_once` reduces the obligation to distinctness of the declared positions of the shared entity list, a conversion (`declared_conv`, rules `isabelle_shared_declarations`) computes them from the list's spine, instantiating at each entity the equation its own constructor names (no name, type or statement is evaluated). The exporter lists a state's declarations in the order of their positions in the name table (`context_items` returns its constants in `string_ord`, the order the table is sorted by; `Symtab.keys` is `fast_string_ord`, length first), every other entity keeping its place. So the declared positions strictly increase, and `declared_once` proves distinctness by one comparison of each adjacent pair: HOL's `strict_sorted_iff` (`sorted_wrt (<) xs ⟹ distinct xs` over a linear order) and `sorted_wrt2_simps` with `sorted_wrt.simps(1)` (the adjacent-pair rules of `sorted_wrt (<)`, the order's transitivity by `transp_on_less`), each `x < y` between numerals closed by a numeral-only simpset. A state whose positions do not increase fails the proof: it is a defect of the exporter, and there is no fallback. The comparisons themselves take 0.017 s at the machinery's 210 declarations; the proof took 1.9–3.1 s there when it was quadratic. `declared_once` instantiates `isabelle_shared_declared_once` at the three arguments of the definition's right-hand side, proves the premise there and transports the theorem to the defined constant through the definition's equation by congruence (`Thm.combination`), so the context term is never rewritten into the goal; the definition's unfolding (0.25 s at the machinery, 0.04 s at the seed) is gone, and what remains is the spine conversion and the comparisons (0.01 s). The conversion reads the constructor at the head of the list and rewrites that entity once, instead of trying the equations in turn (task 279): 0.204 s to 0.107 s at the machinery's 340 entities, 0.0153 s to 0.0071 s at the seed's 80, measured held, three rounds, the same declared positions. Ordering the statements' equations first is slower still (0.22 s): at both states the declarations outnumber the statements (210 to 130, 60 to 20). The dispatch reads each equation's constructor from the element its left side takes and keep-or-drop from whether its right side is a Cons, and instantiates the chosen equation at the entity's arguments and the rest of the list (`declared_bind`, `declared_instance`), with no match, rewrite or beta conversion: 0.107 s to 0.0008 s at the machinery, linear at 0.002–0.003 ms an entity (measured held at 7, 80 and 340 entities). `Conv.rewr_conv` ends in a full beta normalization of its right side, which here walked the rest of the list at every step. A consumer at a state `S=(roots,NAME_context)` takes `isabelle_declared_once (snd S)` from it by unfolding the state. `define_state` also notes `NAME_names_distinct` (`distinct (fst NAME_context)`), `NAME_positions_closed` and `NAME_roots_closed` (every entity's and every root's positions below `length (fst NAME_context)`) and `NAME_roots_distinct` (`distinct NAME_roots`), kernel-checked: distinctness of the names from their strict increase, each adjacent pair compared from the first differing character by one rule per bit (`isabelle_literal_less_bit0`–`_bit6`, over `isabelle_char_bits_less`; a shared character by `isabelle_literal_less_same`, a proper prefix by `isabelle_literal_less_empty`, each instantiated by name with no type inference), chained by the structure's `increasing`, the chain `declared_once` uses with a numeral comparison; positions by per-constructor conditions (`isabelle_shared_term_below`, `isabelle_shared_entity_below`, `isabelle_nodes_below`, and `list_all` for the lists) instantiated at each constructor (`below_conv`), each distinct numeral comparison proved once, type positions read through nodes that read only earlier nodes (`isabelle_table_type_below`); the roots' distinctness from the positions of their constants, read by one instantiation per root (`isabelle_shared_heads`, `isabelle_shared_roots_distinct`) and compared as numerals. Held: the context facts 0.30 s to 0.14 s at the seed and 1.09 s to 0.60 s at the machinery; the whole-simpset comparison of differing characters had been half of the names' chain. Every state the exporter defines is then `state_presentable` by one instantiation of `Development_State_Presenter.state_presentable_exported` at these four facts (`Development_Machinery.development_states_presentable` at the seed and the machinery). |
| Isabelle_Renaming | Isabelle_Entities, Inference_Embeddings | A correspondence moves every name of a context to another position while the table holds the same name there. Each reading — positions, constants, equation heads, subjects, declarations, reachability, and the whole assessment and acceptance — is the image of the original reading, so no decision depends on a position or on table order. Reversal is one concrete correspondence; the dependence of an equation reading on the fixed base names is exposed, not hidden. |
| Isabelle_State_Difference | Isabelle_Renaming | Two states read from different checked contexts correspond through the names both tables hold: each position of the first table is embedded at the position of the same name in the second, injectively when the tables have no repeated name, and as a correspondence when the second holds every name of the first. The removed and added entities of the second state account for both states completely, and a state read against any renaming of itself removes and adds nothing. The correspondence of two checked states through the names both tables hold, and their removed and added entities; the difference accounts for both states completely and is empty against any renaming of a state (`isabelle_state_renamed_unchanged`). The read-state locality of a theory change in the stage-1 gate is an instance of this notion. |
| Isabelle_Local_Names | Isabelle_State_Difference | A value presented with the names it uses: local names and the local embedding through the existing table embedding, invariant under every correspondence of tables (`isabelle_local_embedding_renamed`, `isabelle_local_entities_renamed`); renamings compose and the identity fixes a value; a table extended by the names it lacks (`isabelle_appended_names`) and the embedding back from it (`isabelle_state_embedding_back`); a root presented by the same notion (`isabelle_local_root`, through the one-entity wrapper `isabelle_local_root_entities`, invariant by `isabelle_local_root_renamed`); the embedding of a table free of repeated names into itself fixes its positions and every entity and term inside it (`state_self_embedding`, `state_self_embedding_entity`, `state_self_embedding_term`, the first two moved from `Development_State_Presenter`); distinct roots inside such a table have distinct local presentations (`isabelle_local_roots_distinct`, from `isabelle_local_root_compared` and `state_self_embedding_term`); and the comparison of local presentations across two states whose tables differ, not only by a correspondence: for positions in range and a second table without repeated names, two one-entity presentations are equal exactly when the second entity is the first moved by the table embedding (`isabelle_local_entities_compared`), and likewise for roots (`isabelle_local_root_compared`), with its helpers `isabelle_local_names_in_range`, `isabelle_state_embedding_named` and `isabelle_name_position_member`. The comparison is what the verdict's removed, added and roots fields consume. Stated once for publication loci and payloads, repaired requests, native answers and packets. The presentation of a value with the names it uses, stated once (moved out of `Development_Publication` at its third use), with the identity and composition of renamings and the table extended by the names it lacks. `isabelle_local_names_agree` (two tables that agree on the names at the positions a value uses give it the same local names and the same local embedding there), whose instances are `isabelle_local_entities_agree` and `isabelle_local_root_agree` (moved here from `Development_State_Edit` and `Development_Native_Answers`); `isabelle_equation_left_agree` stands beside it, proved by its own recursion, since the equation reading reads names directly and not through the local names; `map_filter_agree` moved here with them (and on to `Map_Filter_Lists`, task 227). The notion `isabelle_name_reading read positions`, a reading that depends only on the names at the positions a value uses; its condition `agree` is its first law, and its second, `appended`, keeps every reading of a value in range under `isabelle_appended_names`, through `isabelle_appended_names_prefix` (with `isabelle_appended_names_longer` and `isabelle_appended_names_nth`). Its instances: `isabelle_local_names_reading`, `isabelle_local_embedding_reading`, `isabelle_local_entities_reading`, `isabelle_local_root_reading`, `isabelle_equation_left_reading`, `isabelle_entity_subjects_reading`; the roots' corollaries `isabelle_local_root_appended` and `isabelle_local_roots_appended`. `entity_row_kept`, `state_edit_contract` and `development_native_answer_state_presentable` cite them; an edited or appended state's later readings cite `appended` rather than proving the prefix again. |
| Isabelle_Name_Trees | Isabelle_Local_Names, Tree_Map_Indexes | The name table read through its index (task 269): `isabelle_name_tree` (bulkload of the table's positions with its names), `isabelle_name_carrier_index` and the interpretation `isabelle_name_index` (the tree map's index through `carrier_index_through_key`, carrier `isabelle_name_at`), `isabelle_name_tree_lookup(_fun)`; the index extended by appended names, the tree's update: `isabelle_name_tree_insert` (from `tree_map_updates`), `isabelle_name_tree_extend`, `_extend_lookup`, `isabelle_name_tree_appended`. | Factor_Finite_Ground_Evaluation | Factor_Finite_Ground_Source, Factor_Finite_Native_Evaluation | Every clause an installed ground source places at its entry is an alpha variant of a placed exact recognizer, without premise or variable, so native evaluation answers every demand at the entry of any installed ground source. | Isabelle_Code_Equations | Isabelle_Entities | The two statement readings of an entity that the shared subject reading does not distinguish: the executable-content reading (the proposition of a code equation, refusing definitions, specifications and declarations) and the definition reading (`isabelle_definition_proposition`, the proposition of a kernel definition, moved here from the repair at its second use). Reuse (REASONING_REUSE.md, "Judge an answer by what its kind may replace and must state"): The entity language's code-equation reading, separate from the subject reading that a definition of the same constant shares. It refuses every other entity kind and states nothing about the equation it returns. |
| Development_Problems | Isabelle_Entities | A problem is a structural subject of the state — the constants it concerns — with its contract, origin and authority; it is presented outside the state as its row in its context (`Development_Row_Data`), and nothing here presents it. Dependencies are ordinary finite inference rules whose rows fire only once their own problem is answered, so settlement is the least closure of answered problems with settled prerequisites and readiness is computed from the actual premises; ready problems are pairwise independent. Problems without a subject, problems reaching undeclared constants and residual problems are retained as computed observations. Settlement is corrected: a dependency row fires only once its own problem is answered. Before, a problem without prerequisites was settled with nothing answered, so a problem depending on it was ready before its prerequisite had an answer; on the seeded state, which has no prerequisites, readiness is unchanged. Ready problems are pairwise independent (`development_ready_independent`). The term of a contract (`development_contract_term`) stands beside the contract: the kind is its constructor, the term what every kind carries. |
| Development_Loci | Native_Path_Stores, Development_Problems, Factor_Finite_Payload_Literals | A locus is a path and nothing else: a role prefix, a kind prefix and the key of the constant the row is about, in that order (`development_locus`). No program compares a kind or a role, and the kind prefix reads a contract's constructor and nothing inside its term (`development_kind_path_cases`, `development_kind_path_inert`). Each part is determined by the locus (`development_locus_role_determines`, `_kind_determines`, `_key_determines`), and the key assignment is a parameter of which only injectivity on the constants in question is asked (`development_locus_injective`), so nothing here waits on the state that assigns it. A problem, its issue, its request and its answer stand at one locus under four role prefixes (`development_locus_shared_tail`). The locus of a problem is stated exactly where its subject is one constant and nowhere else (`development_subject_constant`, `development_problem_locus_at`, with `_exact`, `_absent` and `development_without_subject_has_no_locus`), the partiality the state already retains; a presented notion's row locus is its `the` (`development_located_at`, with `development_located_at_subject`), and the term of a contract, which a row carries inert beside the kind its locus carries, is `development_contract_term` (stated in `Development_Problems`, beside the contract). The presented locus is `path_term` of its bits, whose only octet anywhere is the empty payload, read by the octet audit's own `Factor_Finite_Payload_Literals.finite_term_payloads` on its executable presentation `finite_path` (`development_locus_payload_octets`), formed and injective (`development_locus_term_formed`, `development_locus_term_injective`). The first of the entry's four restated contracts, which every later one instantiates: rows are keyed by a locus, the request search descends it, the presenters carry it. It decides the form of the parts, never their meaning. The declared parts of an answer are read by Isabelle's outer syntax with the keywords of the context the answer is framed in, never by the host: tokens, commands and their kinds are Isabelle's own (`Token.explode`, command tokens, keyword tokens), so the reading follows every command the frame's imports declare. It refuses by form alone: commands outside the declared kinds, attributes and targets in declarations and statements (they would act beyond the answer's own constants once its theory is adopted), a proof that continues past its lemma, an equation that is not one string token, a method that runs ML, and control or document antiquotations. The meaning of the parts stays the verdict's. A name the definitions introduce must not already name a constant or a fact of the frame (`Sign.intern_const`, `Global_Theory.intern_fact`), since the adopted answer would change what it reads as in every importing theory. A checked state as the relational skeleton the verdict of a kind reads and no more: constants as atoms keyed by a path and carrying their names inert, entity rows in one family per kind of the entity language and one family for the roots, and `declared`, `subjects` and `mentions` as citations of those atoms with the statement inert. A kind is the family that holds a row, never a datum inside it, so the verdict's two kind arguments are selections of families (`kinds_present`). `state_presents` carries every condition as a premise and computes none; three consequences are derived here, each with the owner of its premise named: the verdict's ninth field (`state_presents_distinct_names`), whose premise the answer's reader owns, as it must refuse a duplicated name; distinct roots (`state_presents_distinct_roots`), whose premise the roots' exporter owns; and the vacuity of the assessment's unknown positions (`state_presents_unknown_positions`), owned by the presentation itself. `keyed_agree` is one notion for keys equal exactly when their values are, instantiated at the atoms by name and at the rows by identity; `keys_shared` puts two presented states over one set of atoms, and `state_presents_renamed` carries a presentation across a table correspondence by consuming `isabelle_local_entities_renamed` rather than proving that invariance again. |
